Afya Analytics HMIS — Screen Content Reference

This document lists the visible content of every screen in the Afya Analytics HMIS (version 3.0) web application: the headings, form fields, buttons, table columns, choices, on-screen text and messages a user sees. It was compiled from the application's page templates with markup, scripts, styles, navigation and footers removed. It covers 710 screens, printouts and panels across 19 modules.

Each entry names its module and, where known, its page address (the part of the web address after the site name), its place in the sidebar menu and the permission a user needs. In a page address, a name in curly brackets such as {id} stands for a record number.

Sign-in and account access

Forgot your password? (screen, Sign-in and account access)

Screen in the Sign-in and account access module. Page address: /auth/Forgot-Password.

Headings and sections — Forgot your password?

  • Forgot your password?

Fields — Forgot your password?

  • Email or Username
  • Enter your email or username
  • Select your organization
  • OTP Code
  • 6-digit code

Buttons, tabs and actions — Forgot your password?

  • Back to Login
  • Send Reset Link
  • Use a different email or username
  • Send OTP
  • Verify Code
  • Resend OTP

On-screen text — Forgot your password?

  • Enter the email or username associated with your account and we will send you a password reset link.
  • We'll send a secure link to your inbox so you can reset your password quickly.
  • We found more than one account for these details. Tap your organization below to receive the reset link.
  • We found more than one account for these details. Tap your organization below to receive the OTP.
  • Enter the 6-digit code sent to ….

Messages and validation — Forgot your password?

  • Something went wrong verifying the code. Please try again.

Messages and validation — Psych Intake

  • Restricted record
  • This patient's psychiatric record is not open to you. In an emergency you can request access — it is recorded against your name and reviewed.
  • This patient has been seen before. A readmission starts a new episode linked to the last one, so their previous legal status, dates and discharge reason carry across without anyone retyping them.
  • An immediate risk is flagged. Say briefly what was seen or said, and open a safety review from the Risk tab.

  • SHA holds one prescription per visit and adds no drug to it once it is active. Each drug is then recorded as dispensed when pharmacy hands it over.

On-screen text — Select all charges for this day

  • Please note the following:
  • Updating price of a daily charge or a ward charge will take effect across all days
  • Increasing the units of a daily charge/ward charge will only have it appear out from days after the last day it was charged
  • Decreasing the units of a daily charge/ward charge will remove the charge from days from the days last days
  • To remove a charge from the chargesheet, you can reduce it's quantity to 0 on any of the days or tap the delete button
  • Changes to a daily charge or a ward charge require page reload to take effect: this is done automatically for you though

Fp Service Form (shared panel, Maternal and child health (MCH))

Shared panel in the Maternal and child health (MCH) module. Shown on: /evaluation/pharmacy/process-meds/{visitId}, /evaluation/visit/{visitId}/destination/{destination}, /inpatient/chargesheet/{admissionId}/edit, /mch/fp.

Headings and sections — Fp Service Form

  • Family Planning Service

Fields — Fp Service Form

  • Patient * (example: Type at least 2 characters)
  • Service date *
  • Visit type *
  • 1st time insertion
  • Reinsertion
  • Immediate post partum FP method
  • Received post abortion FP method
  • Notes

Buttons, tabs and actions — Fp Service Form

  • Cancel

Options and choices — Fp Service Form

  • New
  • Revisit

On-screen text — Fp Service Form

  • Client
  • First ever user of a contraceptive
  • (register row 1)
  • not applicable to a revisit
  • Contraceptives issued
  • Register rows 2-11. Tick every method issued at this contact — a dual-protection contact is one visit counted in more than one row.
  • Long acting and permanent methods
  • Register rows 12-17. These are counted by insertion type rather than new/revisit.
  • Insertion type *
  • BTL and vasectomy are permanent — the register has no reinsertion cell for them.
  • Removals
  • Rows 18-19
  • Post partum and post abortion FP
  • Rows 24-26
  • MOH 711 section D

Messages and validation — Fp Service Form

  • previous FP … at this facility
  • most recent
  • so this defaults to Revisit.
  • No previous FP contact recorded here, so this defaults to New. Tick "first ever user" only if the client has never used a contraceptive anywhere — these records cannot see other facilities.
  • This client's record has no gender, so every method is being offered. Set the gender on the patient record to narrow this form.
  • Age band … ( … yrs)
  • register rows 20-23, taken from the patient record.
  • This client has no usable date of birth, so the contact cannot be counted in the register's age bands (rows 20-23). It will still be counted in every method row.
  • The register only counts this in the NEW column, so it does not apply to a revisit.

On-screen text — Weekly Slots

  • a slot recurs every week until it is ended.
  • No weekly slots yet. A slot is a standing arrangement — "this patient, this chair, every Monday, 08:00–12:00" — and the bookings are generated from it.
  • Creates dated bookings from every active weekly slot. Safe to run more than once — a booking that already exists is left alone, and anything that would clash is reported rather than double-booked.
  • Stop this standing arrangement? Future bookings already generated from it are cancelled; past ones and anything already checked in are kept as a record of what happened.

Messages and validation — Weekly Slots

  • created, … already existed.
  • skipped for conflicts:

On-screen text — Diagnosis & staging

  • Primary tumour
  • Site and morphology are ICD-O-3. Free text is rejected — behaviour is stored separately, so " … / … " is assembled for display and never parsed back apart.
  • Basis of diagnosis — drives everything downstream
  • Registry reporting, SHA claim eligibility and the right to approve a curative plan all key off this field and the confirmation date — not off first contact.
  • Revision … is on file. Saving writes a new revision; the existing one is kept exactly as it is.
  • A restage is a new row with a reason, never an edit of the old one.

Messages and validation — Diagnosis & staging

  • Terminology not yet bound
  • Site and morphology are ICD-O-3. Everything else here is a local code system until the terminology workshop signs off. No guessed codes ship.

On-screen text — Toxicity & response

  • Grade
  • Serious adverse event

  • mm
  • Sum of diameters … mm from … mm
  • The verdict is derived from the measurements. It can be overridden only with a reason, and both are stored.
  • Plan continues unchanged. A partial response keeps the current line. Progression would close this plan and open a second-line one — offered, never automatic.
  • Next assessment scheduled. The protocol sets the interval; the date is an offset from this assessment.
  • Nothing is claimed twice. The imaging behind this assessment accrues once, against the CT tariff.
  • No adverse events recorded on this plan yet.
  • Worst grade per term per cycle. A decision aid built from the recorded events — editing a cell is not possible, because a wrong cell always means a wrong source record.

On-screen text — Treatment plan

  • Build a treatment plan
  • A plan is the primary clinical axis: diagnosis, intent, line of therapy and a protocol version, from which the cycles are projected.
  • A diagnosis is required before a plan can be built.
  • These inputs are stored WITH the plan, not referenced from elsewhere. A weight change later re-derives the next cycle's dose; it never rewrites a dose already given.
  • Counters follow the member, not the facility. A transfer must carry them.
  • Approving freezes the protocol version ( …) and the parameters below onto this plan. After approval the clinical definition cannot be edited — a change means closing this plan and opening another.
  • A plan is closed, never deleted. Closing as completed materialises the surveillance care plan from the registry, anchored to the last cycle actually given.
  • Member-wide · checked … ago

Messages and validation — Treatment plan

  • Curative intent needs a verified diagnosis
  • This diagnosis is recorded as …. A curative plan requires cytology or histology. Record the confirmation on the diagnosis, or close this plan and open one with palliative intent.
  • of entitlement would remain for imaging, supportive care and radiotherapy.
  • Approval requires the oncologist role. The approving user, the protocol version and the whole parameter set are frozen onto the plan at that moment.
  • Cross-facility figure is
  • The cap follows the member, not this facility. … An approval made now can commit treatment that will not be paid for.

  • No attendance recorded.

On-screen text — Administration

  • Single-operator administration
  • Only where the facility has explicitly enabled it. Every such event is flagged for governance reporting.
  • No protocol drugs resolved for this cycle.
  • Ordered and given are separate columns, always.
  • Stored with the administration, so the dose can be re-derived years later. Never recomputed from a current weight.
  • None recorded.
  • Blank means not recorded. Nothing defaults to zero. This record cannot be edited — a correction writes a new revision with an author and a reason.

Messages and validation — Administration

  • The preparer and the verifier must be two different people.
  • Observations, vitals and toxicity can still be recorded. Only the act of giving the drug is gated.

On-screen text — New screening

  • Screening performed
  • last screened
  • Recorded and flagged. This screening stays in the register but is excluded from the routine coverage denominator, so the facility's coverage figure stays honest.
  • Outcome
  • Negative — schedule next screen
  • Next due is computed from the registry interval
  • Only available on a negative result — this screen is recorded as ….
  • Treat at this facility
  • Where the lesion qualifies — this visit, or a session booked below.
  • A negative screen has nothing to treat.
  • Treatment session
  • Refer out — tracked referral
  • A referral is an object with an expected return, not a printed note. It lands on the defaulter list if the patient does not come back.
  • Repeat — unsatisfactory sample
  • The sample failed, so the screen did not happen. Eligibility re-opens sooner than the routine interval.
  • Consent & messaging
  • Screening never creates a diagnosis. Only histology does.

Messages and validation — New screening

  • No clinical content in messages
  • Reminders never name the programme, the result or the diagnosis. No consent, no message — filtered in the query, not in the sending code.
  • You do not have permission to record screenings.

On-screen text — Referrals out

  • past expected return
  • Patient
  • Traced x …, no contact
  • No referrals for this filter.
  • A referral closes only when a result is written back against it. Printing the note, or the patient simply attending, does not close it.
  • Tracing ladder
  • Day 0 — referral created. The expected date is set from the receiving facility's route turnaround.
  • Expected date − 3 — reminder. SMS with a date and a place, never the reason.
  • Expected date + 7 — CHP assigned. Community health promoter for the patient's unit picks it up.
  • Expected date + 30 — lost. Marked lost only after three recorded attempts, each with who and when. Never automatic.

On-screen text — Morgue record

  • day … in storage
  • Location
  • Identity
  • The death
  • Handover
  • Personal effects, as received
  • This is the list written at the door. Custody of each item is tracked below.
  • Storage history
  • Every stay, including moves out for viewing or an autopsy. This is what makes a fridge fault reconstructable afterwards.
  • Here now
  • This body has not been placed in a space.
  • The tag, source and admission time cannot be changed — the register is indexed and reconciled on them. Every correction is logged with its reason.
  • It stays on the record as cancelled, with the reason. Another can be raised against this body afterwards.
  • The bill
  • Storage accrues daily on a schedule, whether or not anyone opens this page.
  • Storage
  • Procedures
  • Outstanding
  • Total
  • Waived
  • A postmortem ordered by the police is not billed to the family; the same procedure on the next body is.
  • Amount:
  • The line stays on the record and is excluded from the bill, so the waiver can be reported on.
  • Property held with the body
  • One row per item, each signed for. A body is not released while anything here is still held.
  • against ID
  • Disposed
  • No property is being tracked for this body.
  • Name at least one item.
  • Why the body is leaving
  • Permit
  • A … cannot be completed without a permit number. It can be added now or at the handover.
  • An unclaimed disposal runs on the facility's own authorisation — record it in the notes.
  • Who is collecting
  • This is the person the approval is given against. Check the identity document now — it is what the record will be read back against if the release is ever questioned.
  • Next of kin
  • if different from the collector
  • Transport
  • Approved for collection by
  • Check this against the document the collector is holding before continuing.
  • Carried over from the release. Correct it if a different vehicle or funeral home turned up.
  • Witness
  • Whoever saw the body leave. Separate from the approver — the approver signed the paperwork, the witness was at the door.

Messages and validation — Morgue record

  • Not in a space. This body is recorded but cannot be found by the board — place it as soon as one is free.
  • Released with property still in custody
  • No space is free.
  • A body is cut open on somebody's authority, and the record has to name whose.
  • The admission form recorded these as received, but none of them are being tracked yet:
  • Raising a release does not hand the body over. Somebody else has to approve it first, and the handover is recorded separately.
  • The mortuary still holds … belonging to this body: …. Sign … over on the Property tab before the handover, or the release will have to be completed with a recorded override.

Receive a body (screen, Morgue)

Screen in the Morgue module. Page address: /morgue/reception. Menu: Morgue > Mortuary > Receive a body. Permission: morgue.admit.

Fields — Receive a body

  • Patient who died
  • Visit (optional)
  • Name (Recorded as it stands at admission — the register keeps this even if Reception later corrects the patient record; Use 'Unknown male/female' if the body is unidentified)
  • Sex
  • Age
  • National ID / passport
  • Date of death (Leave blank if unknown)
  • Time
  • Place of death
  • Type of death
  • Cause of death (provisional)
  • Police OB number (example: OB/12/03-10-2026)
  • Police station
  • Brought by
  • Phone
  • Relationship
  • Condition on arrival
  • Personal effects (What came in with the body. Each item's release is tracked separately.; example: Type an item and press enter)
  • Space (Can be left blank — the body is received now and placed when a space is ready.)

Buttons, tabs and actions — Receive a body

  • Receive body

On-screen text — Receive a body

  • Where has this body come from?
  • This decides what else is needed — a ward or theatre death is linked to the patient who died; a body brought in dead has no record here and needs a police OB number.
  • Who is the deceased?
  • The death
  • Police report
  • The OB number is the thread back to that report — without it the body cannot be accounted for to anyone who asks later.
  • Handover
  • Where will it be kept?
  • free of

Messages and validation — Receive a body

  • No space is free. The body can still be received — record it now and place it once a space is available, rather than leaving it unrecorded at the door.

  • Rate card
  • What the mortuary charges. Storage is per day; procedures are per event.
  • per
  • Current
  • No rates are set. Nothing will be charged until one is.

Messages and validation — Morgue settings

  • No storage rate is set, so no body is accruing storage charges. Set one below — the daily job backfills the days it skipped once a rate exists.
  • Setting a price does not change existing bills. The rate it replaces is closed the day before this one starts, and charges already raised keep the price they were raised at.

Psychiatry (screen, Setup and configuration)

Screen in the Setup and configuration module. Page address: /core/setup/psychiatry. Menu: Setup > Psychiatry Settings > Psychiatry. Permission: psychiatry.settings.manage.

Headings and sections — Psychiatry

  • Which forms this clinic uses
  • Patient-facing wording
  • Safety gates
  • Timings
  • Needs your clinical lead first

Fields — Psychiatry

  • What this clinic is called in reminders and printouts (Never the clinic's real name. This goes out by SMS and home in a bag.)
  • Crisis line (Printed on every aftercare plan.)
  • Programme name
  • Block a psychiatric admission until its legal form is complete (The system records the pathway and checks it is complete. It never decides the admission — the admitting officer does.)
  • Block discharge until the discharge summary is signed (Off: a missing summary is a warning on the discharge request. Keeping a patient on a ward over paperwork is a decision, so this is off by default.)
  • Raise the triage colour when risk is recorded as high (Raises the visit to Very urgent. It never lowers a colour another clinician set.)
  • Hours of written notice before a voluntary patient may leave (MOH 613 and MOH 637. The statutory value is 72.)
  • Hours within which an emergency admission must arrive (MOH 638. The statutory value is 24. Arriving later is recorded and flagged, never refused.)
  • How long emergency access lasts (minutes)
  • Minutes before an unacknowledged urgent alert is escalated
  • Remind staff when a legal review falls due (Off until the statutory deadlines are confirmed. The dates are shown on the record either way — a reminder on a deadline nobody has verified would be this system asserting a legal requirement it invented.)
  • What may be shared with SHA (Deny until your clinical lead approves a sharing rule. Claims still go through; only clinical content is withheld.)

Buttons, tabs and actions — Psychiatry

  • Save settings

Table columns — Psychiatry

  • Collateral information (form 03)
  • AWARE relapse-risk scale (form 06)
  • Family therapy session (form 14)
  • Group therapy sessions
  • Counselling consent
  • Rehabilitation forms
  • Occupational therapy
  • Readmission (form 17)

On-screen text — Psychiatry

  • Switching a form off hides it. Records already signed on it stay in the patient's history.

Messages and validation — Psychiatry

  • These are facility settings. They change how the module behaves here and nowhere else, and several of them are deliberately conservative until your clinical lead has confirmed otherwise.
  • Psychiatry settings saved

  • This looks similar to an existing procedure, but the match isn't certain. Map it to one of them, create it as a new service, or skip it.
  • No procedure in the catalogue matches this name — create it as a new service, or skip it.
  • Create as a new service
  • (added to the catalogue)
  • then priced)

  • Insurance:

On-screen text — Biological Reference Ranges

  • Download the template — reference parent_procedure / procedure and age_group by name, adding (ID) — e.g. Full Haemogram(41) — only when two records share a name. Set type to range (needs lower + upper), less_greater (needs lg_type + lg_value) or text (needs text_reference). Rows that already exist are skipped; any invalid row rejects the whole file so nothing is half-imported.
